| R-COMP-READY-03 | 2026-11-14 | Owner: 「Firefox 應該是沒有用了，你可以幫我把它從印象檔中移除，沒有關係。」 Firefox ESR came with the base image and layer 9 only hid its menu entry. The members' browser is Google's Chrome (Chromium on arm64) and the driver's is Chromium, so a third browser was only a place an owner would sign in for nobody (R-COMP-SIGNIN-01). Image layer 10 purges the `firefox-esr` package (with the `libevent-2.1-7` only it needed), and with it its `x-www-browser` alternative, its `/usr/bin/firefox` diversion, its menu entry and its mime registrations; `/etc/firefox-esr`, `/usr/lib/mozilla` and XFCE's own `/usr/share/xfce4/helpers/firefox.desktop`, which the base put there and no package of Firefox owns, are removed by name; the user menu entry layer 9 wrote for it is no longer written. The two scripts of the image that still named it (the fallback at the end of `raphael-browser`, and the workspace's profile folders and lock files) are rewritten in the image by that step, not in `desktop/` or the step that wrote them, because a change there makes every later step run again and a PC that built layer 9 would download Chrome and the packages anew. Chromium (the driver's, and the members' on arm64) and Google Chrome are untouched; every way to a browser (the launcher, the menu, XFCE's Web Browser, `x-www-browser`) still opens the members' own. Layer 9, a member's own computer and the six-screen one every member shares, is named in `vm.known_previous` and made again on layer 10 at its next start, workspace kept (a `.browser-profiles/mozilla` folder an existing workspace has stays; nothing reads it). The image is no smaller: the base's own layer still holds Firefox's files, and a later layer cannot take them out (measured: 4.17 GB before and after). `npm run verify -- --computer` requires that no Firefox ESR package, program, entry, configuration or mention is left in any computer ("N screens browser mark") and starts a layer 9 computer of each shape on layer 10. Stated limit: removing the files from the base's layer would need the base image rebuilt, which this repository does not own. | pytest, computer acceptance | hard | core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_firefox_esr_is_purged_and_nothing_that_opens_it_is_left; core/tests/evaluate/test_computer_acceptance.py::test_browser_mark_reads_the_launcher_the_helper_the_menu_and_what_the_web_browser_opens; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_the_shared_computer_of_layer_seven_is_made_again_on_the_next_layer; core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_layer_five_is_a_previous_layer_only_in_the_shape_it_was_released_in |
| R-COMP-SIGNIN-01 | 2026-11-13 | Owner, signing in to Google at a member's computer so that his members have his account: "Couldn't sign you in. This browser or app may not be secure", a yellow bar about `--no-sandbox`, and "Restore pages?". The members' browser is one a person signs in with. A browser started with `--remote-debugging-port=0` tells every page it is automated (`navigator.webdriver`), which is what Google refuses: the seat browser is started with `--disable-blink-features=AutomationControlled`, never with `--enable-automation` or headless. `--test-type` keeps the unsupported-flag bars away (the seat browser is started from its binary and never read the Debian wrapper's flags file). A computer is stopped by ending its processes, so before the browser's first start its profile is marked as closed (`exit_type` Normal) and the browser is started with `--hide-crash-restore-bubble`; a Preferences file that is not what the browser writes is left as it is. The same step keeps passwords, cards and addresses out of the profile (`credentials_enable_service`, the password manager and autofill off): every member reads that one profile and a password the browser keeps can be shown again on its own page, so what an owner types at a seat is not offered to be saved ("Save password?" was, in the first real run on Chrome); sign-ins stay as cookies and saved values belong to the vault. The same holds as the browser's own managed policy, whatever a profile says or whether it could be written, and by that policy the browser is never signed in to as a browser and never syncs: Google's own Chrome offers an owner who signs in to Google on a page to turn on sync, and his own saved passwords, history and bookmarks would come down into the profile every member reads (found in review before any owner signed in). `npm run verify -- --computer` reads the running browser's own flags, its profile and its policy and opens a page in it whose title says what `navigator.webdriver` is ("shared browser is a person's"). The shared computer has one browser: its desktop's Browser icon, and a link another program opens, give a window of the members' own browser on the screen the pointer is on, because a second browser with a profile of its own is where an owner would sign in for nobody (a computer of a member's own keeps the desktop's browser, and Google's Chrome there is given the flags Debian's Chromium reads from /etc/chromium.d). Stated limit: whether Google lets a sign-in through is Google's decision at the time; no test signs in to Google, and the owner's own sign-in is the proof. | pytest, computer acceptance | hard | core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_the_members_browser_is_one_a_person_signs_in_with; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_a_profile_whose_browser_was_ended_is_marked_closed_and_any_other_file_is_left; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_the_shared_computers_browser_icon_opens_the_members_own_browser; core/tests/computer/test_seat_tools.py::test_the_members_browser_is_never_signed_in_to_as_a_browser_and_keeps_nothing_typed |
| R-COMP-OWNER-INPUT-01 | 2026-11-13 | Owner: 「明明顯示我正在控制，但我根本就沒辦法控制」 (the enlarged screen of a seat, a sign-in page). Every check of an owner's hold had looked at what the members were refused, none at whether the owner's own mouse and keys arrive. In the build he had, the card's take-over did not leave the watching state of an enlarged screen and the pill read the lease alone; main's pull requests 65 and 67 changed both. The core's own path is proven and kept proven: `npm run verify -- --computer` sends a click and a typed line as the app's page sends them (the session's relay path, on the connection made while watching) and requires that they reach the held seat and reach nothing before the hold or after the handback ("shared owner input"); the `shared-computer` native journey has a member ask for a sign-in at its seat, takes the seat from the conversation's card and types into the page, and the member continues once. Stated limit: a seat's screen is served by a server that presses exactly the keys it is sent, so a character sent without its Shift (a script's "@", capitals from a Caps Lock that was on before the view opened, another keyboard layout) is not typed there; a computer of a member's own adds the Shift itself. | pytest, computer acceptance, native journey | hard | core/tests/evaluate/test_computer_acceptance.py::test_shared_acceptance_drives_real_tools_through_every_numbered_check |

## Computer image, layer 6 (2026-11-12)

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-COMP-PICTURE-01 | 2026-11-12 | Owner: 「現在的桌布不知道為什麼跑不出來…桌布是原本那個利姆露的照片好了」. On layer 5 the desktop was one flat colour for two reasons, both measured in a real container. The session started a watcher of its own without the owner's picture, and the core, finding a watcher running, left: the picture was copied into the computer and never shown. And the image had no gdk-pixbuf loader for SVG, so the town (five SVG drawings) was never drawn either. The core is the one place a watcher starts: it ends the one before it and starts its own, with the owner's picture when there is one (R-UI-95) and the town when there is none; the session starts none. A picture that was changed, removed or put back is on the desktop at the computer's next start, with no restart of the computer or the app; a restart that ended the watcher starts it again. The image installs `librsvg2-common` and its build fails without the loader. `npm run verify -- --computer` reads the screen: the picture's own two colours while there is a picture, many colours (the town) and never one flat colour once there is none, the picture again when it is put back, and exactly one watcher. | pytest, verify --computer | hard | core/tests/computer/test_vm.py::test_a_changed_or_removed_picture_is_put_up_at_the_next_start_of_a_running_computer; core/tests/computer/test_vm.py::test_start_installs_the_decorative_wallpaper_once_per_start_and_target; core/tests/computer/test_wallpaper.py::test_the_session_starts_no_watcher_of_its_own; core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_image_installs_screen_views_and_preserves_the_single_token_port; core/tests/evaluate/test_computer_acceptance.py::test_the_desktop_picture_verdict_tells_the_picture_the_town_and_a_flat_colour_apart |
| R-COMP-KEYS-01 | 2026-11-12 | Measured in a real container: the desktop had four workspaces and every default key, and `ctrl+F2` moved the whole computer to another workspace, so every window was gone from the screen; `ctrl+F4`, an ordinary "close this tab", did the same. A computer has one workspace, and no key acts on the whole desktop: the workspace keys, show desktop, log out, lock, xkill and the display settings are unbound for any number of screens. With more than one screen the keys that walk over every screen's windows (alt+Tab and its kin) and every key that starts a program (it opens where the one pointer is) are unbound too. A window's own keys stay (close, maximize, move, resize, tile), and one screen keeps alt+Tab and its program keys. On one screen, keys that cannot be changed are reported and never hold the screen back. With more than one screen, every removal is attempted despite individual failures, then the helper reads the bindings and workspace count again; remaining shortcut_removals bindings, a workspace count other than 1, or a failed required query raises into the existing retry before a new ready mark is written. The two session-default override waits retain their 20-second limits. `verify --computer` presses ctrl+F2, ctrl+F4 and ctrl+alt+d on a real computer with a window open and finds it shown where it was. | pytest, verify --computer | hard | core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_one_screen_loses_the_keys_that_act_on_the_whole_desktop_and_keeps_its_own; core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_several_screens_also_lose_the_keys_that_reach_every_screen; core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_the_keys_are_taken_away_once_the_session_has_copied_them_and_one_workspace_is_left; core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_keys_that_cannot_be_changed_never_hold_the_screens_back |
| R-COMP-XLOSS-01 | 2026-11-12 | Measured in a real container: a one-screen computer whose X server was lost never got it back, kept its ready mark, answered `ready` for good and listed no window, because only a clipped view (more than one screen) died with the server and told the helper. The helper looks at the X server itself, for any number of screens, without starting a process; when it is gone the mark goes, the computer is not called ready, and the product's next start restarts it. `verify --computer` ends the X server of a one-screen and of the largest computer, sees the mark go within 5 seconds and the status turn not ready, then starts the computer and finds an X server, the mark and the panel. | pytest, verify --computer | hard | core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_a_lost_x_server_takes_the_mark_away_for_any_number_of_screens; core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_the_look_at_the_x_server_starts_no_process |

The repair still happens once and is still counted. | pytest | hard | core/tests/computer/test_lifecycle.py::test_self_repair_is_once_in_the_current_turn_and_never_replays_uncertain_input; core/tests/computer/test_lifecycle.py::test_viewer_tunnel_gets_one_retry_and_counts_the_repair |
| R-COMP-LAYER-01 | 2026-11-12 | A computer of layer 5, the shape every released computer has today (one screen that says its count, 3 GiB, two CPUs), is a previous layer exactly like 3 and 4: its next start or update rebuilds it on the new image and keeps its workspace, and erasing it needs no new image. Any other shape under that label stays incompatible and is never removed. `verify --computer` makes a fixture of each released layer whose image is on this PC (4 and 5), from the image itself and under the run's own owner, and starts, updates and erases it with the product. | pytest, verify --computer | hard | core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_layer_five_is_a_previous_layer_only_in_the_shape_it_was_released_in; core/tests/computer/test_lifecycle.py::test_previous_computer_maintenance_rebuilds_without_starting_old_image; core/tests/evaluate/test_computer_acceptance.py::test_previous_fixture_matches_release_shape_without_changing_vm |

## Computer image screens, stage A (2026-11-11)

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-COMP-SCREEN-01 | 2026-11-11 | Owner: 「每個 bot 配一台電腦的功能要一模一樣」. Layer 5 can give one container 1 to 6 real outputs, each with a panel and its own clipped view through a screen token on the one published port. One screen uses the main VNC server directly. Readiness requires `/dev/shm/raphael-screens.ready` on the fresh-per-start tmpfs: the mark says this boot's screens are set up, stays while a view or the helper restarts and goes only when the layout is lost, and a viewer reports a refused connection itself. Tokens stay under `/run/user/1000`. Supervisor never gives up on the helper, and a running computer whose driver is healthy but whose mark is still absent after 20 start polls is restarted once under the repair guards. A computer of a previous layer (3 or 4) is this owner's own for stop, restart, update, repair, reset and member-data deletion before it has ever been upgraded. A `Target` refuses a screen outside 1..screens..6, and the layout request has one 15 s deadline. The helper starts `/usr/bin/X0tigervnc` in the foreground, ends leftovers before views start for any count, and waits boundedly for all six ports to clear. Listener checks parse `/proc/net/tcp` without VNC connections. Supervisor lacks CAP_KILL and only restarts what exits; the helper ends its own children, and nothing may call `supervisorctl stop` or `restart` in a computer. A new layout uses a 1x1 update request then moves the pointer to screen 1 only after success; matching monitors skip both. Multiple screens disable only dock shadows, writing only when different without a panel restart. Existing per-bot and shared targets remain screen 1 of 1 in stage A. Member assignment, leases, browser changes and the default mode are deferred. | pytest | hard | core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_grid_has_only_requested_screens_and_the_full_canvas; core/tests/computer/test_screens.py::test_des_published_known_answer_vectors; | R-COMP-PERF-12 | 2026-11-05 | Shutdown/idle/eviction stop without removing workspace or volumes; recognized image rebuilds announce preservation first. The team's shared computer is never removed. Since 2026-11-11 (owner, after deleting a member in the app: 「如果選擇刪除他的電腦資訊也會卡住，而且理論上不會問這個問題，應該是知道他可能有這個東西，會自動刪掉，如果還沒開起就不用顯示，因為根本就沒有他自己的電腦，要盡量減少顯示在前端的事情，不要讓使用者需要思考太多不是他們該思考的東西」) deleting a member removes its own computer data too, by itself: the owner is never asked, waits for nothing and sees nothing of it (no toast, status or card, in the conversation or anywhere). The member and all of its own are deleted first and `members.delete {id}` answers at once; afterwards, in the background, its computer goes (the container, then the folder on this PC or the volume on its host), but only if the app made one for it (a record in `computer_data`, or its folder here): a member without one contacts and starts no engine and records nothing. When the engine or host cannot be reached the removal stays owed in the database (migration 80, written in the deletion's own transaction) and is paid at the first answer of that engine, on the computer monitor's tick and at the core's start; Docker Desktop is never started to delete data. Every external step has a time limit, a failed try is logged without personal data and counted (six from the timer, a host that does not answer at most every ten minutes; an engine coming back tries again), a computer in use waits without being counted, and data already gone is success. The primary member's `member_delete` tool, behind its yes card, takes the same path. Native speed work owns its random container and exact cleanup and reports five-run median/worst budgets. | pytest, vitest, look, authorized native | hard | core/tests/members/test_deleted_member_computer.py::test_a_member_that_never_had_a_computer_costs_the_engine_nothing; core/tests/members/test_deleted_member_computer.py::test_the_computer_goes_after_the_answer_and_the_owner_is_asked_nothing; core/tests/members/test_deleted_member_computer.py::test_an_engine_that_cannot_be_reached_owes_the_removal_until_it_is; core/tests/members/test_deleted_member_computer.py::test_an_engine_that_never_answers_never_holds_the_deletion; core/tests/members/test_deleted_member_computer.py::test_a_computer_still_starting_for_the_deleted_member_does_not_hold_the_deletion; core/tests/members/test_deleted_member_computer.py::test_a_remote_hosts_computer_and_volume_are_removed_on_that_host; core/tests/members/test_deleted_member_computer.py::test_the_owed_removal_survives_the_core_ending_and_is_paid_at_its_start; core/tests/members/test_deleted_member_computer.py::test_the_primary_members_deletion_through_its_tools_removes_the_computer_too; core/tests/computer/test_smooth_computer.py::test_erasing_never_touches_the_shared_workspace_or_a_container_that_is_not_ours; app/src/main/member-actions.test.ts::asks nothing about its computer, reads no settings and sends only the member; member-delete-ask-computer; computer-speed |

Since 2026-11-11 (owner: 「有連續兩張卡片的時候，它好像顯示不出來…理論上是要一張卡片顯示出來就好，然後就可以一次把三個都刪掉之類的…就不要分這麼多卡片，讓我去按同意」) the firm changes a member asks for in one turn are not asked one behind another (the second was not even proposed until the first was answered, and a card nobody saw ran out): they are held until the turn is in and asked as one card, a line each, one yes and one no. In the 全部允許 mode only the firm ones are asked, as before. The yes is for exactly that list (its digest and its lines), each change is carried out in order under its own part of it and once, one that fails does not stop the others and is said plainly to the owner and to the member, and a change proposed after the card was shown is a card of its own. ## Owner-PC commands (2026-11-01)

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-PC-01 | 2026-11-13 | Owner: 「正常對話的時候，不會有操控我電腦的邏輯，你可以看一下我跟拉菲爾的對話，他會一直想要操控我電腦，所以我們直接拿掉就好了…操控我電腦的邏輯只會在快速問答的部分，就是我按下 Alt+Space 的時候才會有這個權限」. Replaces the rule of 2026-11-01 that sent every member to the owner-PC command tool first. The owner's own PC (the local command tools, the run on his desktop, the fixed commands that open, lock, copy and change the volume) is in reach of a quick question and of nothing else: not an ordinary conversation, typed or said, Raphael's or a member's, not a room, an exchange between members, a message from a phone, a task or a routine, and no permission mode, 全部允許 included, reopens it. Such a turn is offered none of those tools, its prompt says nothing of that PC, the fast router (which could pick a command or a desktop run) is not asked to route it, and a call that names one anyway is refused. Work that needs a computer goes to the member's own computer, and when that is not available the member says so and never falls back to the owner's PC. A quick question is unchanged. | pytest | hard | core/tests/agent/test_owner_pc_reach.py::test_an_ordinary_turn_is_offered_nothing_of_the_owners_pc_and_nothing_it_tries_reaches_it; core/tests/agent/test_owner_pc_reach.py::test_a_cli_brain_in_an_ordinary_turn_has_no_tool_or_word_of_the_owners_pc; core/tests/agent/test_owner_pc_reach.py::test_the_snapshot_holds_the_owners_pc_only_when_it_is_told_it_is_in_reach; core/tests/agent/test_owner_pc_reach.py::test_no_permission_mode_lists_the_owners_pc_for_a_task_or_a_routine; core/tests/agent/test_owner_pc_reach.py::test_a_quick_question_still_has_the_owners_pc_and_the_words_for_it; core/tests/agent/test_owner_pc_reach.py::test_open_chrome_on_my_computer_in_an_ordinary_thread_goes_to_the_members_own_computer; core/tests/computer/test_local_commands.py::test_the_owners_pc_is_out_of_reach_of_every_turn_that_is_not_a_quick_question; core/tests/members/test_local_commands.py::test_named_member_cpu_question_has_no_local_command_and_no_card; core/tests/background/test_local_commands.py::test_a_task_that_asks_for_a_local_command_is_told_there_is_no_such_tool |
| R-PC-02 | 2026-11-13 | Local commands have shared ask (default), always and never modes. Since R-PC-01 only a quick question reaches them, and a quick question can show no card, so a command runs there only where nothing waits for a yes (全部允許), and never stays off. The first-consent card (Always allow, Allow once, Never, Deny once), the chat approvals without a timer and the 30-minute background approvals were built for conversations and tasks, which no longer have these commands: they stay in the code and have no door until the owner decides whether a quick question asks at the overlay. Settings no longer has a control for the mode (owner, 2026-11-14: 「本機指定我覺得應該可以先拿掉，先拿掉好了，如果我需要補上的話再跟你說」): the stored value keeps its rule for a quick question, the card has no way into Settings and the words that sent an owner there are gone from every language. Always still asks for dangerous/opaque commands and turns that consumed outside content, secret stores are refused, and only this PC approves. Exact commands stay on cards and out of public timeline text and audit payloads (R-AG-13). | pytest, vitest, look, authorized native | hard | core/tests/computer/test_local_commands.py::test_always_still_asks_for_dangerous_and_subscription_commands; core/tests/computer/test_local_commands.py::test_secret_stores_are_refused_even_after_explicit_approval; core/tests/agent/test_owner_pc_reach.py::test_a_quick_question_keeps_the_rule_it_had_for_a_local_command; app/src/main/pages/settings-view.test.ts::Safety has no setting for local commands: only a quick question reaches this PC; local-command-settings-gone |
| R-PC-03 | 2026-11-01 | The core owns each noninteractive, hidden UTF-8 PowerShell process tree, closes stdin, filters the worker environment, bounds output with head/tail, enforces timeout and isolates background job output/stop by conversation or task. Stop/app close kills descendants. CLI subscription commands use the same approval card. Model-supplied cua-driver session IDs are neither offered nor forwarded. | pytest, authorized native | hard | core/tests/computer/test_local_commands.py::test_adapter_filters_environment_and_kills_on_timeout_and_cancel; core/tests/computer/test_local_commands.py::test_the_session_is_raphaels_and_never_the_models |

Public paraphrases of decisions dated 2026-10-20 through 2026-10-25. Later rules take precedence. Each row is one requirement. Explicit conflicts remain `human` with both versions. `hard` violations fail judge regardless of score; `human` needs product/native acceptance. This ledger records requirements, not claims of compliance. Mechanical references use `repository/path::exact test title`.

Home was removed on 2026-11-12 (R-UI-114, R-UI-43, R-UI-03 and R-UI-04 are retired): the owner asked for the app to open on Raphael's
conversation and for the calendar to open only when asked (R-NAV-02, R-SIDE-02). The 2026-11-07 and 2026-11-08 designs of Home below are
kept only as the ledger's history.

Since the owner's later note of 2026-11-07 the guide is one card of a designed size in the middle of the window, no progress bar and no side, after Grok Bot's first run: each step is one calm centred column, a title with two small aria-hidden slimes floating by it (gone below 640px wide), the choice, and under the choice two round buttons together in the middle of the card: at the left the way back and at the right the way on, each a 48 px circle with one filled triangle that points where it goes (the same drawing, turned round for the way back), 12 px apart. Neither has words, so each is named for assistive tech and in its tooltip: 上一步, and 下一步 (the last step's way on is named 開始使用 and holds a check instead of a triangle: it finishes, and nothing comes after it). The way on is a circle of the solid accent; the way back is on a quiet grey, one step lighter under the pointer; neither has an edge; the way back takes the app's focus ring and comes before the primary in the tab order. The pair stands in the same place on every step, never stacks and is one control tall, so the step's content keeps the room (the guide's own sizes are the one exception to the shared button height, kept by the owner on 2026-11-08). How it came to this, all on 2026-11-11: the owner sent back the two stacked pills (「導覽這個 button…上下堆疊會造成我們畫面有些空間比較緊，像是選擇 AI 工具，還有連接你的服務這邊會比較需要空間。感覺左右放，或者左右放那個箭頭之類的，或許會比較好」); then the first answer to that, a round arrow beside one wide pill in the middle (「這個不是這樣做，我要的是左右 button 為甚麼會設計成這樣 我要最好的 UI/UX」); then the second, a worded pill at each end of the row (「改成集中在中間這邊好不好？然後 icon 改一下，可以改特別一點點，不一定要寫字，can 用兩個三角形之類的」). The welcome is its one wide pill in the middle and nothing else (owner, 2026-11-14: 「第一頁應該只會有開始就好了。它那個叉叉現在是因為我已經導覽過了，所以才會有叉叉，那基本上一般的使用者不會看到這個」). The guide is the first run's only: Settings has no entry that opens it again (owner, 2026-11-14, once he had checked the first run: 「我剛剛確認過啟動都沒有問題，所以你可以把設定裡面的導覽拿掉 不用留著了」). The tools (accounts, the key, the providers) and the services (under a search box) are tiles in a grid of three where it fits, each one row since 2026-11-09 (owner: the tiles were too tall and an email broke in two): a mark in a soft square, a name over one quiet line that never wraps, its control on the right inside the tile, and a black edge with a check when it is on; a service tile that only signs in is one whole button. Without a Composio key the services step offers Composio's card first and then only the apps that need none; its search finds every app, n8n included, and sets it up in its tile or the store's own form. The abilities are Settings › 工具's own rows and dialogs, each saying which service it uses and opening the choice of service, key and model. Controls are real buttons and switches. | R-VC-03 | 2026-11-11 | Owner, open since 2026-10-26 (「用 Alt+Space 問問題時，回答期間 Alt 仍不會開始聽寫」): the dictation key always wins. Once the words of a question said to the overlay (Alt+Space) are in, it goes on as a turn of its own, so Right Alt starts a dictation at once while its answer is worked on, waits for a yes or no, or is on the overlay, exactly as if nothing were there; the model call is never cancelled by it, and stopping the question stays what it was (Esc or the overlay's Stop, which also drops an answer that waits). While the owner dictates the overlay shows the dictation alone: an answer that arrives meanwhile, or was showing when the recording began, is held, and once the dictation is finished and its words are typed it is shown for its ordinary time. If a newer quick question was asked meanwhile only the newest answer shows; every answer is in the quick conversation as ever. A held answer is kept in memory only, so it is dropped when the app quits or the core restarts. Right Alt stays ignored only while the question itself is recorded or transcribed (the core has one session at a time), and Alt+Space does not bury a yes or no question that waits. The spoken yes or no, its 30 seconds, the recording limit and the learning of corrections do not change. | test, native | hard | app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::dictation_works_while_a_question_said_to_the_overlay_is_answered; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::dictation_works_while_a_question_said_to_the_overlay_waits_for_a_yes_or_no; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::an_answer_that_arrives_during_a_recording_is_held_and_shown_once_its_words_are_typed; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::an_answer_on_the_overlay_when_a_dictation_begins_is_shown_again_after_it; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::a_newer_quick_question_replaces_the_answer_that_is_held; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::the_answer_of_an_older_quick_question_never_follows_a_newer_question; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::a_stopped_quick_question_shows_nothing_whatever_it_answers_later; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::esc_stops_a_held_answer_after_the_recording_that_took_the_overlay; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::the_core_restarting_drops_an_answer_that_is_held; app/src-tauri/src/dictation.rs::a_held_answer_is_dropped_when_the_app_quits; overlay-held-answer; features/dictation.md |
| R-VC-04 | 2026-11-14 | Owner, with 每日總覽 (an HTML page) open in Raphael's conversation and the app's own voice button pressed (「如果你打開 HTML，然後按下 app 內部的語音輸入的話，那個拉菲爾大師會被 HTML 擋在後面，所以我們看不到我們到底有沒有在說話…拉菲爾大師還是會顯示在最前面，他永遠是最前面的」): the indicator of in-app voice is the voice mode's flyer (`voice-mode.ts`, `.ra-flyer`: the corner's own emblem on a canvas, listening and brightening with the voice, a child of the conversation's stage). A page open in the thread's place hides the thread and, with it, the "hearing" line the thread draws once the recording is over, and `page-view.css` put away every child of the stage but the thread, the page and the composer, so the flyer was `display: none` the whole time. Now the flyer is the one child that stays: it stands in front of the page, centred above the composer (never on it), while the owner speaks; when the recording ends and a page stands, it is held there (`VoiceMode.hold`), turning as the corner's emblem does while words are heard and made into a message, and goes back when their turn comes, the 120 s wait ends, the dictation is cancelled or fails, the page is put away or the conversation changes. It takes no pointer over a page, so the page scrolls and its buttons work under it, and the composer's button and Escape end or cancel the recording as before; the 1.5 s "did the app start listening" timer no longer takes a held flyer down. Without a page nothing changes (the flyer flies back at the end of the recording and the thread's hearing line shows). It is the same indicator, never a second design, and at rest nothing moves. | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/voice-mode.test.ts::the page's stylesheet puts away every child of the stage but the thread, the page, the composer and the flyer; app/src/main/voice-mode.test.ts::with a page open the flyer stays after the recording and leaves when the words are in; app/src/main/voice-mode.test.ts::without a page it is not held: the flyer goes back as it always did; app/src/main/voice-mode.test.ts::a held flyer goes back when the page is put away, and a new press listens with the same one; voice-over-page-listening; voice-over-page-hearing; voice-over-page-gone; voice-flyer-emblem; features/dictation.md |

## Member computer hosts (owner, 2026-11-01)

R-COMP-HOST supersedes the shared-desktop default: every member lazily receives a
separate computer and browser profile. Sign-ins remain per member. A team folder at
`/home/cua/team` is shared; members sign in separately. Settings offers one shared
location dropdown (This computer / Another computer), with an SSH config alias for
the latter. A configured alias selects the remote engine; otherwise installed local
Docker is used. With neither, the rest of the app works and the existing computer
settings explains the missing runtime and offers installation or another host.

Remote host check (owner, 2026-11-11; R-COMP-HOST-01). "Check host" reports the truth about what a real start
needs, and for every failure says which of these is missing and shows the one command or config snippet that fixes it,
copyable, with no wizard or new screen: an alias in this PC's ssh config (`ssh_alias_unknown`: a `Host` snippet to
fill in), the host key accepted once (`ssh_hostkey`: `ssh <alias>`; `ssh_hostkey_changed`: `ssh-keygen -R <host>`),
key login without a password prompt (`ssh_key`: `ssh-keygen -t ed25519` and `ssh-copy-id <alias>`, or the PowerShell
pipe on Windows), a reachable SSH server (`ssh_unreachable`), Docker installed (`docker_missing`), `docker` on the
non-interactive PATH (`docker_path`), Docker running (`docker_stopped`) and usable by that account (`docker_access`).
A reachable host with a running Docker and no image yet is healthy: the real start pulls and builds the image on the
host's own engine, so `image_pending` is a note ("the first start prepares it, roughly 5 to 15 minutes"), never a
failure and never a command. An image built for another architecture (`architecture`) is the only image failure, and
its fix removes that image on the host. Every command shown is pasted on this PC, reaches the host through
`ssh <alias>` or sets up this PC's own ssh, and never builds anything from the app's sources, which an installed app
does not have. Docker Desktop on a Mac cannot be installed over ssh, so that class shows words and no command.

Remote Docker uses a validated `ssh://alias`, no published viewer port, named
workspace/team volumes on that engine, and a temporary random loopback SSH viewer
forward. It never starts Docker Desktop on this PC. Local idle-stop remains 15
minutes; remote desktops have no idle-stop. New v4 containers have a 3 GiB memory/swap cap
(not a reservation) and two CPUs. Local starts keep at most two running owned computers, counting
old incompatible containers without stopping them. Only a compatible, longest-idle
computer with no work or owner lease may be evicted; otherwise
report capacity. Remote hosts have no such limit.

The member viewer has one dark neutral frame in both themes, the member's name,
and a collapse control at the top right. It preserves the current stream and
handback nonce. The image has a plain wallpaper and one small bottom-centred dock
with Browser, Files and Terminal. No browser starts with the desktop. Task teaching
is outside scope. AT-SPI and websockify stay until real driver/viewer compatibility
can be established.

## Connect apps as Grok Bot's, and one colour for everything that floats (2026-11-12)

Owner, 2026-11-12, comparing Grok Bot's Connect Apps with ours: 「再來是 connectors，它進去之後，Grok bot 功能不太一樣的是它不會分，就不會像我們現在分那個已連結跟商店，他們都在一起的。我覺得這樣設計比較好一點點，就它可能就是推薦你。那也不會顯示它現在有幾個，可能不會顯示 1741 個還是什麼。他就會上面是放你已連接的，然後下面可能就放一些推薦的。然後推薦的話也會像是我們現在商店一樣去分，就可能會分全部、溝通、生產力、開發之類的。你可以看一下他們是怎麼做的，這樣感覺比較乾淨一點」, and then, on the colours: 「然後你可以看到每個 connector 跟 顏色設計都不太一樣 我感覺可以把 dialog 都設計成這個顏色 他們感覺是有一套規則的」.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |

The light theme keeps its character: a white surface, a #e5e5e5 edge, a light grey control (#ececec) and hover (#eaeaea), a black chosen chip with white words. The surfaces say it once (`:where(.menu, .modal, .mb-dialog, ...)` in styles.css points the ordinary tokens at the floating ones inside them), `--menu` is `var(--float)`, and a new floating surface joins that list and uses `--float` and `--float-edge`. Pages, the sidebar, the thread, the composer and a toast (the inverse of the page) keep their own colours. Every text grey keeps 4.5:1 on `--float`, `--float-ctl` and their hover and pressed fills, the status colours and the delete question's red hold on them (check-palette.mjs), and a text button on a floating surface is framed by its fill (R-UI-18). | vitest, lint, look | hard | app/src/main/floating.test.ts::every floating surface stands on the floating tokens and says it in one scope; app/src/main/floating.test.ts::the tokens are Grok Bot's measured values in the dark theme and a white surface in the light one; scripts/check-palette.mjs; connect-apps; connect-apps-more; member-delete-ask; context-menu-member; composer-model-small; member-maker; automations-detail-own; model-choices-answer; profile |

| R-FLOAT-03 | 2026-11-14 | Owner (2026-11-14, with a picture of Grok Bot's Settings in the dark theme: 「grokbot 他們的設定或是其他的 dialogue，明明就是比較深的顏色才對啊，你自己看一下。它應該是三層顏色，最外層最深 ( dialog 外 )，再來是內層，再來是內容。你這邊可能要確認一下」): a dialog is three layers in both themes. Outermost and darkest, the window under the scrim (`--scrim`; #040404 in the dark, Grok Bot's #050505); the middle, the dialog itself with its nav (`--dialog`: #121212 in the dark as Grok Bot's Settings, #f5f5f5 in the light, a quiet off-white); the lightest, the cards and groups on it (`--float`: #1f1f1f with a #2b2b2b hairline in the dark, white with a #e5e5e5 hairline in the light). The order of lightness is the same in both themes, outside < dialog < card: the light theme mirrors the model by dimming the outside, not by turning it round. A control on a card is `--float-ctl` (dark #282828 as Grok Bot's pills; light #ececec so that it still reads on the off-white dialog), a hovered row `--float-hover` (dark #292929; light #eaeaea) and the selected nav row `--selected`. A menu, a popover and a tooltip are on the card layer, `--float`, with an edge and a shadow (Grok Bot's menu is #1f1f1f with a #292929 edge): over a dialog they are lighter than it, over a card they are told apart by their edge. The dialogs are `.modal` (Settings, Connect Apps and an app's page), `.mb-dialog`, `.cs-dialog`, `.av-crop` and the member search (`.menu.side-search`); `--content` inside `.modal` is the dialog's own colour (a sticky bar stands on it), `--panel` the card, and a box that had only a line (`.cs-box`, an app's information and tools) gets the card fill. Measured on Grok Bot (2026-11-14): only its Settings has the three layers; its Connect Apps, member search, About, Feedback and Shortcuts dialogs are one #1f1f1f surface with the same #050505 outside. The owner asked for the three layers in every dialog, so `--dialog` is one token: set it to `--float` to give Connect Apps and the rest Grok Bot's single surface. ## Conversation-managed connections (2026-11-01)

R-MCP-CHAT-01: a custom MCP server is an editable conversation card. Public configuration and secret names may be proposed; values cross only the existing masked secret card into the vault. Initialization and discovery must succeed for the exact configuration before owner enable. Stdio testing uses the existing local-command consent and setting; allow-all skips yes, never a forbid, disabled local commands or secret-store protection. Saving a secret alone does not claim the task resumed. Stop rejects late discovery.

R-MCP-CHAT-02: connections_list and manage_connection use the existing connection and connector services. App/tool enable, disable, disconnect and reconnect apply to API, authenticated CLI MCP and background offers and to the check immediately before execution. A stale alias receives a refusal explaining the new state. Disconnect follows the shared mutation approval. No extra Settings page or preference is added.

Offline evidence: core/tests/integrations/test_chat_connections.py, core/tests/agent/test_connection_management.py, core/tests/background/test_connection_offers.py and app/src/main/mcp-proposal.test.ts. Selected real-entry scenes and limitations are mapped in features/connections-mcp.md. These rules extend R-AG-35 and R-OWNER-02.

## Shared conversation cards (2026-11-02)

| ID | Date | Rule | Verification | Level | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R-COMP-SEAT-01 | 2026-11-12 | Owner: 「每個 bot 配一台電腦的功能要一模一樣」, as Grok Bot: "ONE computer for every member, each on its own screen, signed in once". Shared mode means one six-screen container on each selected host, with a durable first-use seat per member. Shared is the default on this PC's Docker since 2026-11-12 (owner: 「跑過就改」, after a real two-member run in the app); nothing in the app chooses the mode, because a feature the owner asked for has no switch. On a remote host (computer_ssh_alias) the default stays a computer per member until shared mode has been run on a real remote host: LocalVm.mode is the one place that decides, and a saved computer_vm_mode always wins. Per-bot retains its own container and lease behavior. A member's own get_browser_state that names no window (nothing at all, the browser's process alone, or ids this member never bound) reads the one browser window on its own screen, and the reply names the real target and tabs: one browser serves every seat, so its process names no window, and real members at a seat sent exactly these and gave up when told their own window was not on their screen. It is that one read only: input still names its window, a target the member did bind is judged as before, and what the core reads for a member (a private page's state, a field before a saved value is typed) is the tab it asked for or nothing. A process alone, on any other call, is answered with what to pass, not with "not on this member's screen". Seat keys keep tools, routes, leases, sessions, active work, holds and private pages separate. Unit identity owns physical locks, lifecycle, capacity, activity and caches. Any seat's active work, hold or busy lease protects physical actions. Every native driver call and every call dispatched with delivery_mode=foreground takes the unit input lock. Other seats' background page calls, dialog inspection, reads and commands continue during an owner hold; foreground page input waits then returns computer_busy. The held seat refuses all member calls. A seventh member selects the least recently used idle seat under seat_lock, revokes its old holder and disconnects its driver and viewers; it publishes the new holder on that exact screen only after a successful window list shows no window touching it other than the desktop's own (the screen's panel and the one desktop window that spans the whole canvas, which are never closed) and those of a screen another member holds now (by its centre such a window is that member's own, never a leftover: a browser's frame reaches 16 px past its screen's edge, and it was closed when a colleague sat down beside it), or the computer is known absent or stopped. A returning member using a cached route cannot use that screen while cleanup is pending. A failed or cancelled transition attempts to restore the old holder to its exact screen, with its own last-use time (so the next attempt still takes the idle member's screen, not a busier one's), before unlocking; a failed restoration reports seat_restore_failed. Any resulting free screen still needs a fresh empty-screen proof before assignment, so no durable quarantine is required. All busy seats yield computer_busy. Status and previews never assign seats. Shared containers use 6 GiB, 4 CPUs and six screens, upgrading known one-screen shapes without removing home or team mounts. Member deletion holds seat_lock through disconnect and window cleanup before releasing seats on all hosts, rechecks the holder before each close, and erases only historical per-bot computers. Viewer opens and session entry points recheck the member, Target and current holder after awaits and revoke displaced sessions before authorizing access. Shared datasets use .raphael-data/members/<core member key>/ for databases, locks, imports and charts, with one common venv and install lock; the public schema, description and results stay unchanged, and the core adds the key only after checking the member's arguments. The runtime requires a string matching [0-9a-f]{16}, refuses symbolic member directories and refuses paths resolving outside .raphael-data/members/. Shared teaching accepts the member's own available seat and observes only the seat: its own window list as the wall projects it (one driver list_windows on the seat's connection) and its clipped RFB picture, through the core's input/tick; it has no relay observer and never calls a canvas-wide observation (get_accessibility_tree, get_desktop_state). Private titles, private-page marks of the seat, a peer's marked window touching the screen, a payment hold and any observation failure withhold text and pixels and record typed keys as private_input. An owner's press waits for that observation before it reaches the computer. Per-bot dataset paths and teaching calls remain unchanged. The core classifies every offered driver argument, including nested properties, in SHARED_ARGUMENTS and refuses unreviewed arguments, desktop coordinate frames, target, from_zoom, capture_id and screenshot_out_file in shared mode. Every named or bound native window is checked against fresh canvas rows and must fit wholly inside its seat before reads or input; a centre-owned straddling window is moved and resized first, otherwise refused. Native gesture points and the full interpolated drag stay inside the seat. Page target bindings are revoked when their native window leaves the seat, including private-picture and vault reads. In both modes, successful target/tab snapshots and active-tab binds remember the exact tab per computer target until disconnect. Every private-picture read and per-field vault read/type uses that target and tab; an unknown tab refuses filling and keeps private pages protected, without guessing or changing member-facing fill parameters. Window lists, placements, verify_state bounds and pointer prose use seat coordinates, while driver tree/token prose is preserved and page JSON appears once. run_steps requires a nonempty live-schema expect for a verify_state observation before any batch action; legacy predicates is a reply field, not an input alias. Desktop pictures come only from the seat's authenticated clipped RFB view. launch_app moves new windows matching the reply's pid or launcher_pid. Only if none match and exactly one other new window appeared does it move that one as a helper fallback; all other windows stay. That fallback can still misattribute a lone concurrent peer window, and matching pids do not distinguish simultaneous same-process peer windows. The seat browser launcher selects now-minus-before windows with this launch's unique title and browser pid. The computer owns Chromium and its profile; per-seat driver sessions attach with existing_profile and never launch it. Displacement gently closes every window touching its screen except the desktop's own and confirms emptiness within a bounded time (LocalVm.seat_clean_seconds: every list and close is a command through the container boundary); an unconfirmed cleanup restores the exact old holder and yields computer_busy. Every free-screen allocation must pass the same check, skipping unconfirmed free screens and never cleaning an existing holder. Owner takeover checks only that seat's non-command activity and the unit native lock, while other seats' reads, page calls and commands continue. Layer 7 (the seat browser launcher and the existing-profile grant, on top of layer 6's picture, keys and X-server look) preserves known layer 3/4/5/6 mounts on rebuild. Clipboard, files and sign-ins are common; shell and browser still run as cua. In shared mode the shell has no screen: foreground and background vm_exec run without DISPLAY; open a window with launch_app. Members are the owner's own: deliberately restoring display access from the shell is outside the seat wall's accidental-input boundary. Not given and never claimed: the core's check and the driver's action are two requests to one X server, so a window that moves or grows by itself between them is not caught (second review, C2-01); launch_app moves a window that is not provably its own only when it is the single other new window. A private mark's way out can be lost and is then kept, never guessed: if a marked window is moved to another seat, its marker reads the old page target meanwhile (which drops that binding) and the tab is then closed, neither the closed-tab reply nor another site can be seen for that mark, and the marker's pictures (and those of a seat the window still touches) stay withheld until the core restarts (fifth review, C5-01). A class name is not an ownership boundary: a member that names its own window after the desktop's is outside the wall. A saved value is never typed into an embedded frame, so a form whose fields live in one (a payment provider's card frame) is the owner's to fill. Before a shared computer's first browser of each start, per-bot homes of this owner on this host that the record does not call handled are carried, inside Linux only (the computer's own image, --network none, the old home mounted read-only, ownership set to 1000:1000; the host's Python copies nothing). A home whose computer is busy is left for the next start; a running idle one is stopped, never removed. If the shared home has no browser profile, the profile of the home whose Default/Cookies is newest is carried first, without Singleton* locks and without the browser's caches; only one profile is ever carried. A member's files go to a folder named after the member (its key appended on a collision; an unknown existing folder is never merged), leaving out checkpoints.EXCLUDED, .raphael-data and the record; above 1 GiB of such files none is copied, but the home's sign-ins and datasets still are. Its top-level DuckDB files go to that member's dataset folder, never the venv. The core writes .raphael-carried.json in the shared home; handled or left-in-place homes never repeat, a failure is retried at the next start and after three failed copies the home is left in place. Every browser of the computer waits for the carry until the same moment, a budget below what browser_open allows, counted from the carry's start; the carry itself is not cut short and goes on behind the browser, keeping each old home's guard until that home's copy has ended. A helper container outlives its Docker CLI: one that runs out of its time is hung and is killed by its own name before the guard is released (when it cannot be seen to have ended or to be absent, its home stays closed to its old computer, which is refused as busy, for as long as that core lives); one whose core is closing is left to finish; one an earlier core left running holds its name, and the next core waits for it before asking that home again. What a copy publishes is handed to the computer's user while still staged in the helper's own folder, and folders made on the way at once, before any long copy; no path a member prepared is walked. A profile folder the browser has written to is kept; one its launcher has only just made, still empty, is replaced by the carried one. No failure keeps the browser from starting, and nothing is shown to the owner. Old homes, containers and volumes are never changed or removed. Stated limits: a home left in place stays reachable only on its old computer; when the home with the newest sign-ins is busy at that first start another home's profile is carried; members can write the record (they are the owner's own). The member folders of datasets keep members from colliding, not from each other: every member has the same workspace, user and venv by design, so a member that swaps its folder for a link while its own dataset call runs reaches what its shell reaches anyway (stage D review, D-01). The helper is root only inside its own throwaway container, whose one writable mount is the workspace every member owns: a member that swaps a staging folder's name while a copy runs can make that carry fail, not reach anything else (second review, D2-01). A core that died while a helper ran leaves that home unguarded until the next core's carry reaches it (D2-02). | pytest | hard | ## Three computer levels (2026-11-02)

The rules below supersede the collapse control and plain wallpaper in the earlier
member-viewer paragraph. The guest retains its Browser, Files and Terminal dock.

| R-UI-74 | 2026-11-02 | Presence uses one shared slime at sidebar, header and live-thread sizes. The same liquid body, colour and costume carry every state: idle breathes and ripples, sleeping closes its eyes beneath a crown bubble, thinking sways with one rising droplet, working kneads with alternating landing droplets, searching peers and hops, computer trembles with eyes down, waiting leans patiently, blocked sags and shivers with a sweat drop, error holds a melted puddle and slow drip, done jumps with two droplets, and arriving drops and squashes. No dots, ribbons or exclamation replace or orbit it. Owner attention wins. Transform/opacity motion preserves the cheap idle strips and uninterrupted loops (R-UI-71). Reduced motion retains eleven distinct still poses and visible liquid props, including both working droplets. Props are at least 8% of the view box and stay inside it at 22/28/40px; eyes and costume stay attached (R-UI-78). Public action hovers update in place and never contain raw tool names or arguments; idle has no action hover. Seen failures return to idle. Header hovers open below the face, stay inside the viewport clear of window controls, and add no pointer focus ring. ## Meetings and private work (2026-11-04)

This owner decision supersedes the sequential room speech and room-scope tool-work clauses above.

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-ROOM-01 | 2026-11-04 | Speech offers only handoff, room_post, ask_member and read-only work_status. Self-handoff creates ordinary private work with a separate room report destination. No approval card is projected into the meeting. | pytest | hard | core/tests/background/test_room_meeting.py::test_self_lane_reads_private_context_and_reports_a_short_card_once; core/tests/background/test_room_cli.py::test_cli_speech_never_opens_an_approval_or_calls_a_plugin |
| R-ROOM-02 | 2026-11-10 | Room speech shares two slots, independently of three private work slots and per-member work admission. No model call asks a member beforehand whether it will speak: an address goes to whoever it names; a message that names no one goes to the room's default responder first, and every other member then reads that answer in its own speech turn and speaks or passes. A member the owner addresses may not pass; its pass is the status line no_reply. Ordinary room rounds use one seen-through snapshot. Explicit meetings give every participant a turn in order, even after earlier passes; owner corrections wake all participants again. A reply has no total clock: each attempt has the whole no-progress window (ROOM_STALL_S), a chosen wait before the next attempt is no stall, and an attempt that hears nothing for the window ends the reply as stalled. A reply that does not finish is a status line only: no bubble in its member's name, no close-out in the chief's, and the provider's own words only in private records and logs. Meeting status comes from persisted exchange state. | pytest, vitest, look | hard | core/tests/members/test_room_turns.py; core/tests/background/test_room_attempts.py; core/tests/background/test_room_meeting.py::test_all_speech_slots_run_while_the_same_member_has_private_work; core/tests/members/test_meetings_followup.py; app/src/main/group-chats.test.ts; room-concurrent-replies; room-reply-status |
| R-ROOM-03 | 2026-11-04 | Prompts carry a computed per-member board, pinned chief decisions and messages after the member's successful reading cursor. No stage has two work owners. The UI board opens from one compact pinned row outside the scrolling thread and hides when empty. | pytest, vitest, look | hard | core/tests/members/test_room_meeting.py::test_speech_cursor_sends_only_new_messages_with_the_board; app/src/main/room-meeting.test.ts; room-status-board |
| R-ROOM-04 | 2026-11-04 | Meetings and posts preserve the real member author, never impersonate owner input, and each public card requests at most one digest round. | R-UI-121 | 2026-11-11 | Owner request (2026-11-11, testing the app: 「有時候我們點一些 button，它不會立即切過去。假設我們現在在調整角色的外觀，我點左邊的工程師，它會先在我點下去的時候把角色外觀的 dialog 關掉，但是不會跳到工程師的頁面…點下去反而會把原本的 card 關掉，這邊的邏輯是錯誤的」): a press outside an open menu, popover, picker or card closes it and is delivered to what was pressed, in the same press, as if nothing had been open; it is never spent on closing. A dialog with a scrim is the exception below (2026-11-13). A menu or popover lets the press through untouched (its own pointerdown, focus and click), and a drawer's scrim (the narrow sidebar, the details panel) closes its panel and then gives the element under the pointer the focus and one click, once. A dialog's scrim (Connect apps, Settings, the member creator, the model chooser, the delete question) only closes it and the press is spent (owner, 2026-11-13: 「如果點 dialog 外面就是把 dialog 關掉就好 不用可以直接點到後面的東西」): nothing behind it is pressed, focused or right-clicked, and no selection starts. A menu open over a dialog is closed first and the dialog by the next press, since the scrim hides the page until then. Presses that began inside the panel, a selection dragged out of it, and a press the panel may not close right now (a busy dialog, a calendar editor holding typed words) close nothing and press nothing. A right click outside a menu or popover closes it and opens the menu of what it pressed, if that has one; over a dialog's scrim it only closes the dialog; an 'are you sure' step is abandoned by any outside press, which still goes through. The focus follows the pressed element; Escape and the keyboard are unchanged. One policy in dismissals.ts serves every dialog, menu and popover. The one control that is not pressed is the one that opened the thing, which only closes it (R-UI-124). A frame (a page open in the conversation's place, a computer's screen) is another document, so a press inside it gives the app no pointer event: the app takes the frame receiving the focus with the pointer over it for that press, and closes every panel the frame is outside of while the frame keeps the press (owner, 2026-11-12: 「假設我在內部檔案，然後點選模型，然後我再點選內部檔案，這個模型選擇模型的 dialog 是不會消失的，一定要點側邊它才會不見」). | vitest, look | hard | app/src/main/dismissals.test.ts::a press on a control outside an open popover closes it and the control's handler runs once, with the focus on it; One mechanism in dismissals.ts does it for every opener: a panel records the control the last click activated when it registers (a menu item that opens a dialog stands for the menu's own opener; a list drawn again is matched by its key), and a drawer's scrim takes its opener from its caller. | R-UI-148 | 2026-11-14 | Owner request, after Grok Bot (2026-11-14: 「如果使用者選擇編輯的時候，不要把它拉回到我們的對話框裡面，而是直接在原本畫面上的 Message Bubble 做編輯，然後編輯完就直接重新發布出去，邏輯是一樣的」): choosing 編輯 in the ⋯ of the owner's message makes that bubble itself the editor (owner, 2026-11-14, again: 「我編輯的時候不用特別改那個 message bubble，用原本的 message bubble 下去編輯就好了」): the bubble keeps its colour, shape, padding, type, width behaviour and place, its words are the field (the caret after them) and it grows with them as the composer does, and a small 取消 and the primary 送出 stand under it at its right edge, in no box of their own. The composer is not touched: its draft, files and quote stay. Enter (never while an input method composes) or 送出 sends through the composer's own send, so the call is the same: the edited words, the files and quote the message carried and `replace_turn_id` of the message it replaces, with the same effect on the history. An edit is the same message with new words, standing where it stood (owner, 2026-11-14: 「我傳送之後，訊息就不見了」): the core removes the message and writes the edit with its id and its time, so the edit keeps its place among what came after it (a routine turn posted since stays below it, and the edit is never written at the end of the thread or under the id of nothing), and the member answers it again; the app shows the edit in the message's place while it is on its way, never hides the edit because its id is the message's, never shows both, and does not take the reader to the end of the thread. The brain's resume session and the conversation's summary hold the old words and are forgotten, as in any retraction. A failed send opens the editor again with the words as typed. Shift+Enter is a new line. Escape leaves at once while the words are the original's, and needs a second press within 1.5 s (the app's cancel gesture, 取消 showing Esc and 「再按一次取消」) once they changed. 取消 leaves the message as it was. An editor belongs to the conversation and message it was opened in and is gone when either is. Files of the original that are no longer stored are listed with × and block 送出 until dropped. A group room's messages have no edit. | vitest, pytest, look | hard | app/src/main/pages/agent-members.test.ts::a sent message is edited in its own bubble (owner, 2026-11-14); app/src/main/pages/agent-members.test.ts::an edited message stands where its message stood (owner, 2026-11-14); core/tests/agent/test_message_edits.py::test_an_edited_message_stands_where_it_stood_among_the_routine_turns_that_came_after_it; core/tests/agent/test_message_edits.py::test_an_edit_forgets_the_brains_resume_session_and_the_summary_which_hold_the_old_words; features/conversation.md (message-edit-open, message-edit-long, message-edit-armed, message-edit-sent, message-edit-placed) |

| R-REACT-01 | 2026-11-14 | Owner, after Grok Bot (2026-11-14): 「emoji 只能有一個啦，每個使用者只能會有一個 emoji。然後其實 bots 它是會根據我們的 emoji 然後有給予回應的，所以大部分的時間都是沒有回應的…假設我們給他按讚啊，或者笑臉之類的。但我有一次按到讚，然後他就問我這則回覆有什麼問題，為什麼會比到讚之類的。其實我覺得這樣設計也蠻好的，我就可以快速地跟他說哪邊我不太滿意這樣子」. A message carries at most one reaction of the owner's, where it is stored: the primary key is the message (migration 82 kept the newest of a message that had several), and choosing another emoji replaces it in one write. Every reaction is analysed, at once (owner, 2026-11-14, on the first version, which waited 4 seconds and answered only a fixed list of negative emoji: 「然後不用等4秒還是什麼東西，不要用固定的規則。這邊你可以確認一下 grokbot 是怎麼做的，我記得它每一個都是會去分析的　因為它馬上就會出現「思考中」」): any reaction of the owner's to a reply of a member's own, positive or negative (Raphael too; in a group room only the reacted message's author), starts that member's own turn at once, with no wait and no list of emoji that count: `source` reaction, empty question, started by an internal note that names the emoji and holds the reply, the owner's message and the few turns before as data, on the member's own model with no tool, no other model to fall back on and no message of the owner's. It shows at once as the member's usual presence in the conversation (「思考中」). The member decides, in its own voice and in the owner's language (since 2026-11-14, after 紫苑, a Japanese teacher, asked what was wrong in Japanese: the window's language is named in the prompt and wins over what the member teaches or the conversation holds, unless the owner's own last words are in another; the reaction prompt carries no rule that lets a persona choose, and the task comes after the persona; the rule for an ordinary reply, `REPLY_LANGUAGE`, no longer follows an "established conversation": it answers in the language of the owner's latest message, not a lesson's): ask in one or two sentences what was wrong when the emoji shows dissatisfaction (what the owner liked in Grok Bot: to say quickly what they are unhappy with), say a few words (a brief acknowledgement, the obvious next step), or say nothing, and then the turn is taken out of the thread: no message, card, failure or notice shows that it ran. Nothing is a pass: the member answers exactly (pass), as a member does in a group room that has nothing to add (`is_pass`: any case, brackets, a full stop or bold). The answer format has no field for silence, and the loop sends an empty answer back for words, so the room's own pass is reused rather than a new token. Cost: every reaction is one model call on that member's model (accepted by the owner). A reaction taken back, or changed to another emoji, while its turn runs ends that turn and leaves nothing (a changed one starts the new emoji's), and so does the owner writing in that conversation (their message is not held up). A reaction is analysed once per message and emoji (`reaction_asks`, keyed by kind, message and emoji since migration 83): the same emoji given again is not analysed again, another emoji is; one that was ended or failed was never made, so it is analysed when it is given again. A member working for the owner in that conversation starts none, and the reaction is then not recorded: it is skipped, not queued, because the owner's turn already shows the member at work and an answer to a reaction would come after it, out of its place (analyses of reactions wait on each other, and never count as work for the owner). Never analysed: the owner's own message, the member's own answer to a reaction, a quick conversation, a reply that did not finish. In a group room only the reacted message's own author thinks, in the same thread, through the room's own speech turn (no other member, handoff or @), and not while the thread is being discussed; what it makes is no discussion of the room (not its meeting, not what it is busy with, and Stop wraps nothing up for it). A reaction is metadata, never approval and never the owner's words: no card, draft or question is settled by it, and the analysis is neither a note for the memory nor an instruction. | pytest, vitest, look | hard | core/tests/agent/test_emoji_reactions.py::test_a_message_carries_one_reaction_choosing_another_replaces_it_and_the_same_again_takes_it_back; A stalled CLI is asked again once, after a question's first wait, only while nothing outside was done in the turn (R-CLI-08) and no call was out when it stalled; after an action, or with a call out that may have been made, it is never run again: the turn ends, says that it stopped and what the receipts show was done and what is unconfirmed, and asks nothing to be repeated unchecked. The mandatory part (this turn's request, attachment names, additions, note and records) is never cut and raises `context_overflow` only when it alone outgrows the window or a provider byte limit. What is left is filled in this order: memory and team sections, the conversation summary, the owner's own recent words (newest first, capped), then recent turns newest first, whole. | R-COLLEAGUE-02 | 2026-11-11 | Owner decision, after he saw a new member whose first message was a paragraph ending 「你今天想先把哪件事交給我？」 (「你是沒有用過 Grokbots 的流程，你這個流程做得非常糟糕。像他後來會一步一步去設定你的 bot 才對。不是這樣子問的，他會直接給你一個選擇的表單，然後一件事慢慢設定完成」; 「要盡量減少顯示在前端的事情 不要讓使用者需要思考太多不是他們該思考的東西」): the first message of every newly made member is its own model's one short greeting line (its name, one sentence, no question in it) together with ONE question card, never an open question alone: what it should take on first, three or four options built from what is known (gaps in the other members' roles, ready connected apps, the owner's saved context; when nothing is known a fixed everyday set: mail, schedule and reminders, research, documents) and the owner's own answer. The card is the ordinary `ask_owner` card (R-OWNER-01, R-CARD-02): durable, answerable like any other; the greeting turn may call that tool and no other, on an API or a CLI brain, with no fallback model and no fixed string. A card with no words of the member's own is never shown as the first message: the call is answered in the same turn that the greeting is missing and the model writes it (at most twice, on every brain; live, a Codex model called the card as its first act with nothing written, and the greeting failed), and only a model that still writes nothing leaves a failed greeting. The instruction is hidden. A brain that fails leaves an ordinary failed turn whose 重新產生 writes it again (members.greet) while the owner has not written; a greeting that came through is never written twice. The idle greeting-card clone of 2026-11-06 is gone; a greeting stored with its old local choices still reads as the line it was. | R-COLLEAGUE-08 | 2026-11-11 | A new member sets itself up by cards, one step at a time (owner, 2026-11-11, see R-COLLEAGUE-02). The owner's answer to the first card (a choice or his own words) starts a turn of its own in the same conversation, whose words are the card's and not a message of his: (a) the member saves its job from the answer with `set_my_job`, says in one short sentence what it took it to be, and the thread shows one centred quiet line (「已設定職務：…」, the event line of a learned skill) where that happened; (b) if the job needs an app that is not connected it shows the existing connect card for that one app, with one quiet line under it that nothing is sent or changed without his yes, and a connected app is never asked about; (c) one more question card: which first task, two or three concrete options from the job plus his own answer; (d) it starts the task he chooses. If the answer already names a clear job and task the cards collapse: it saves, says so and starts, with no further card. One card is open at a time, with no progress mark, step count or summary at the end. Anything he types instead of answering is simply his message (the card is closed as its x closes it, the member answers him and does not bring the set-up up again); closing the card (x) ends the set-up quietly with no model call. A member takes a new name only when his answer gives one in so many words: it then renames itself with `member_update` and the thread shows the first Bot's rename line; otherwise its name is never touched. The steps are the code's order and the model's words: the greeting and each answer carry only their own step to the model, and nothing of it enters the everyday prompt. The row has no role label: sidebar rows show no role (R-UI-77). ## Approved computer and storage hardening (2026-11-05 follow-up 1)

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-SEC-SSH-01 | 2026-11-05 | Remote Docker uses a private Unix socket under an owner-only directory, never an unauthenticated TCP or SOCKS forward. Unsupported SSH/Docker clients fall back to per-command ssh:// transport. | pytest | hard | core/tests/computer/test_followup_security.py; core/tests/computer/test_smooth_computer.py::test_ten_remote_docker_calls_share_one_ssh_connection |
| R-SEC-PRIVATE-01 | 2026-11-05 | Filled pages have no screenshot expiry. Each mark belongs to one exact computer/page target/tab and retains its physical computer and original bound native window. On a shared computer, it also withholds another seat's pictures whenever that window overlaps the seat, or the required fresh window list fails; an absent window affects only the marker's seat. Both the pre-call and post-call decisions apply (before the call only for computer tools; after it for anything that returns a picture), each from one window-geometry command, and nothing is listed when there are no peer marks. A complete semantic read of that same tab showing a different HTTP(S) origin with none of the filled refs lifts protection. The private-picture probe also retires that exact tab's mark on the driver's refusal that this tab is gone, in either form it gives (both measured on 0.37.0 and each matched whole): for a browser the seat attached to, an error whose message is "confirmation provider failed: tab <this tab id> no longer has a live CDP page target"; for a browser the driver launched itself (per-bot), a refusal that is NOT an error, code browser_tab_not_found, message "cannot attach to tab target <CDP id>: CDP Target.attachToTarget failed (-32602): No target with given id found". A tab id the driver never knew has the same codes and other words, and keeps the mark. Same-origin navigation, lost fields, incomplete reads, every other error, timeout, disconnect and other-tab reads or binds retain protection. Reads return a value-free semantic tree and explanation. A safe observation only authorizes subsequent captures, never pixels captured before it. | pytest | hard | core/tests/computer/test_followup_security.py::test_private_page_requires_changed_origin_and_absent_fields; core/tests/computer/test_followup_security.py::test_navigation_after_capture_cannot_release_the_captured_private_picture; core/tests/computer/test_login_visibility.py |
| R-SEC-LOG-02 | 2026-11-05 | Rust caps native stderr lines at 16 KiB, discards overlong lines in full, and applies Python-equivalent credential scrubbing before the log sink. Invalid UTF-8 and EOF are handled without exposing partial credentials. | cargo | hard | app/src-tauri/src/core_stderr.rs |
| R-SEC-DATA-01 | 2026-11-05 | Before data/memory directory use, Windows applies a protected owner-SID-only inheritable DACL; POSIX uses mode 0700. ACL failure stops use. Migration backups retain the newest five versions per source and delete only unchanged files recorded as app-created; untracked or owner-edited files remain. | pytest | hard | core/tests/test_storage_security.py; core/tests/memory/test_files.py |

## Report pages (2026-11-10)

| ID | Date | Rule | Check | Severity | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| R-PAGE-01 | 2026-11-10 | Owner request (2026-11-09, after Grok Bot's Page: 「grokbot 甚至可以在內部做報告 挺猛的」): a member's report is a page of the existing page system, given to show_page or update_page as `report` instead of html, checked by the core and drawn by the app in its own design with every word escaped; no model markup or script runs and no frame is made. In the conversation it is the member's file card with the page's icon, its title and 「頁面」; pressed, the report opens in the conversation's place (R-PAGE-03), the composer stays below and the arrow at the conversation header's top left goes back (2026-11-11: it had a × at the page's top right, which is gone). By default it states when its data is from and that it only states recorded facts (the promise stays the default); since 2026-11-14 (owner, of Grok Bot's one line of summary under each page's title: 「你也可以看到 grokBots 跟我們實質性的差距，你可以看一下它怎麼呈現的。他們內部的 page，我覺得他們做得非常的好」) a member may give `summary`, one line of at most 160 characters, which takes that line's place (R-PAGE-07); findings carry status words in the theme's colours, a table has tabs for its groups, and the team is boxes in each member's colour joined by labelled right-angle arrows that cover nothing. The read tool `team_work` returns recorded facts only, in the owner's private chat only. Since 2026-11-10 (Grok Bot's Page has Share at its top right; of saving a file, opening it in the browser or copying it as text the owner chose 存成檔案) the report's one button is 存成檔案. Since 2026-11-12 (owner: 「你那個下載跟分享的 Icon 都要特別設計過 可以看一下 grok bot 怎麼設計的」「位置要特別設計過 外觀也是」) nothing floats over the page: the button stands where a thread's share stands, first of the conversation header's tools at the top right, the same 36 px round button, drawn as a file with an arrow into it and never as the arrow out of a box that shares a member (2026-11-11: 「分享檔案的 Icon…要有所區別」). That one place holds what is on screen, as Grok Bot's share does: on a thread the member's share, whose card is one row with a mark drawn for it (the member's own face twice, the copy behind and above the one that is, no stock icon), on a report its 存成檔案, and since 2026-11-14 (R-PAGE-08) on an HTML page the same button (a plugin's page, which works only through its host, has none). A press saves the report as one HTML file of the app's own drawing in the owner's output folder, or Downloads when none is chosen or it takes nothing, never over another file, selects it in File Explorer and says where it went, and the report stays open. An HTML page is saved the same way as one file that the core builds of the page it keeps (`pages.export`: the window gives its id and no text), without the helper that talks to the window, in its reader's own theme and under the page's own policy in its head (its sources, no connection, no form, no frame), after the same secret scan. Nothing in the file runs or loads: its policy stands first, the core writes it only for a report page and only with that drawing's markup, and no folder comes from the window. It follows its reader's light or dark theme, white on paper, and its team is drawn for the width it is read at. Series take eight chart colours in a fixed order (`--chart-1` to `--chart-8`, the same hues stepped for each theme and checked as a set for colour-blind readers on that theme's page), or each member's own colour when every series is a member (one member's colour among the chart colours could pass for one of them); words keep the text colours. Marks: lines 2 px at any width, an area a light wash, columns at most 24 px and rounded at their end only, the parts of a stack or a pie kept apart by 2 px of the page, a line's last point a dot ringed in the page's colour, a single series' last value written; a single series of columns writes every value and draws no axis, any other chart a value axis of at most five round steps with hairlines; labels along the bottom thin out to five in a half section and nine in a whole one, never cut. A section may take `span: half`: two half sections in a row stand side by side where the page is 640 px or wider, one above the other where it is not; a half section alone takes the width. When no section of a report gives a span (the members' first pages gave none even when told), two charts in a row of at most twelve labels each pair the same way. A report of more than six titled sections lists its contents only beside the page (960 px), never above it. All of it also draws in the shared file, where nothing runs (the file's attributes gained only `preserveAspectRatio`). A member's HTML page was drawn by whatever style the model wrote, and the app gave it only its theme's colours, so pages came out uneven: a table with no lines, a heading as small as the text, a note that was a plain paragraph. The core puts a base style (`agent/page_style.py`, one string, injected by `pages.document` for a page the member writes and not for a plugin's) in front of the page's own style. Readable type (15 px on 1.6, h1 32 px, h2 22 px, h3 17 px, a line at most 44 em), the page's root at most 1120 px wide and centred with a 24 px gutter (16 px under 640 px) whatever the body's margin the page resets, tables with a header row on the surface, hairlines, rounded corners, `.num` right-aligned and tabular on a th and a td, and under 720 px a table that scrolls inside itself instead of widening the page; links in the theme's blue, code, quotes, rules, lists, images and the plain controls. Classes a page may use, named in the tool's words (about 25 of them): `.callout` with `.info`, `.warn`, `.ok` (a tint of the theme's own colour, an icon drawn in CSS, its first heading in the tone), `.toc` (a boxed 「on this page」 list), `.chips` (a row of choices as quiet words, the chosen one bold and underlined), `.cards`, `.kpis`. Every selector stands in `:where()` (so no rule of it outranks a rule the page writes, whatever the order; a pseudo-element, which cannot stand there, keeps the lowest specificity it has), and it speaks only in the theme's words (`--bg`, `--surface`, `--fg`, `--fg-2`, `--muted`, `--line`, `--link`, `--ok`, `--warn`, `--font`) so both themes follow; it names no address, font file or picture, so the page's policy stays as it was.
